## AI Research

### [AI #158: The Department of War](https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/YTnzcZSbA69fMCjNo/ai-158-the-department-of-war)

**来源**: LessWrong - AI

**摘要**: <p>This was the worst week I have had in quite a while, maybe ever.</p>
<p>The situation between Anthropic and the Department of War (DoW) spun completely out of control. Trump tried to de-escalate by putting out a Truth merely banning Anthropic from direct use by the Federal Government with a six month wind down. Then Secretary of War Hegseth went rogue and declared Anthropic a supply chain risk, with wording indicating an intent to outright murder Anthropic as a company.</p>
<p>Then that evening OpenAI signed a contact with DoW,</p>
<p>I’ve been trying to figure out the situation and help as best I can. I’ve been in a lot of phone calls, often off the record. Conduct is highly unbecoming and often illegal, arbitrary and capricious. The house is on fire, the Republic in peril. I have people lying to me and being lied to by others. There is fog of war. One gets it from all sides. It’s terrifying to think about what might happen with one wrong move.</p>
<div>


<span id="more-25141"></span>


</div>
<p>Also the Middle East is kind of literally on fire, which I’m not covering.</p>
<p>Last week, I had previously covered the situation in <a href="https://thezvi.substack.com/p/anthropic-and-the-department-of-war?r=67wny"><strong>Anthropic and the Department of War</strong></a> and then in Anthropic and the <a href="https://thezvi.substack.com/p/anthropic-and-the-dow-anthropic-responds?r=67wny"><strong>DoW: Anthropic Responds</strong></a>.</p>
<p>I put out <a href="https://thezvi.substack.com/p/secretary-of-war-tweets-that-anthropic?r=67wny"><strong>my longest ever post on Monday</strong></a>, giving my view on What Happened and working to dispel a bunch of Obvious Nonsense and lies, and clear up many things.</p>
<p>On Tuesday I wrote <a href="https://thezvi.substack.com/p/a-tale-of-three-contracts?r=67wny"><strong>A Tale of Three Contracts</strong></a><strong>, </strong>laying out the details of negotiations, how different sides seem to view the different terms involved, and provide clarity.</p>
<p>On Wednesday negotiations were resuming and things were calming and looking up enough <a href="https://thezvi.substack.com/p/gemini-31-pro-aces-benchmarks-i-suppose?r=67wny"><strong>I posted on Gemini 3.1</strong></a> and went to see EPiC to relax, and then by the time I got back all hell broke loose yet again when an internal <a href="https://thezvi.substack.com/p/slack">Slack</a> message from Dario, written on Friday right after OpenAI tried to de-escalate via rushing to sign its contract but it looked maximally bad and OpenAI was putting out misleading messaging, came out. It had one particular paragraph that came out spectacularly badly, and some other not great stuff, and now we need to figure out how to calm everything down again and prevent it getting worse.</p>
<p>What’s most tragic about this is that, except for the few exhibiting actual malice, there is no conflict here that couldn’t be resolved.</p>
<ol>
<li>Everyone wants the same thing on autonomous weapons without humans in the kill chain, which is to keep 3000.09 and wait until they’re ready.</li>
<li>With surveillance, DoW assures as it isn’t interested in that and has already made concessions to OpenAI.</li>
<li>DoW insists it needs to be fully in charge and not be ‘told what to do’ and that is totally legitimate and right but no one is actually disputing that DoW is in charge and that no one tells DoW what to do. We’ve already moved past a basis of ‘all lawful use’ or ‘unfettered access’ with no exceptions, including letting OpenAI decide on its own safety stack and refuse requests. It’s about there being certain things the labs don’t want their tech used for. DoW is totally free to do those things anyway, to the extent allowed by law and policy.</li>
<li>If there was an actual drag down fight over this and it’s an actual national security need, the contract language isn’t going to stop DoW or USG anyway.</li>
</ol>
<p>And if DoW and Anthropic can’t reach an agreement, because trust has been lost?</p>
<p>Understandable at this point. Fine. The contract is cancelled, with a wind down period that will be at DoW’s sole discretion, to ensure a smooth transition to OpenAI. Then we’re done.</p>
<p>Except maybe we’re not done. Instead, the warpath continues and there’s a chance that we’re going to see an attempt at corporate murder where even the attempt can inflict major damage to America, to its national security and economy, and to the Republic.</p>
<p>So can we please all just avoid that and do our best to get along?</p>
<p>About half this point is additional coverage of the crisis, things that didn’t fit earlier plus new developments.</p>
<p>The other half is the usual mix, and a bunch of actually cool and potentially important things are being glazed over. I hope to return to some of them later.</p>

<h4>Deepfaketown and Botpocalypse Soon</h4>


<p>Yeah, this doesn’t sound awesome, and it isn’t going to win AI any popularity contests.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/MorePerfectUS/status/2027025917961494821">More Perfect Union</a>: Burger King is launching an AI chatbot that will assess workers’ “friendliness” and will be trained to recognize certain words and phrases like “welcome to Burger King,” “please,” and “thank you.”</p>
<p>The AI will be programmed into workers’ headsets, according to @verge .</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/allTheYud/status/2027331040159711575">Eliezer Yudkowsky</a>: Predictions should take into account that many actors in the AI space are determined to immediately do the worst thing with AI that they can.</p></blockquote>
<p>It was inevitable, it’s powered by OpenAI, and it sounds like it’s mostly going to be a very basic classifier. They’re not ready to try full AI-powered drive thrus yet either.</p>
<p>Chances are this will mean everyone will be forced to use artificial tones all day the way we do when we talk to a Siri and constantly use the code words, and everyone involved will be slowly driven insane, and all the customers will have no idea what is happening but will know it is f***ing weird. Or everyone will ignore it, either way.</p>


<h4>A Young Lady’s Illustrated Primer</h4>


<p><a href="https://www.nytimes.com/2026/03/02/world/asia/china-education-ai.html">China’s parents are outsourcing the homework grind to AI</a>. The modern curse is to demand hours upon hours of adult attention to this, often purely for busywork, so it makes sense to try and outsource it. The question is do you try to make the homework go away, or are you trying to help your child learn from it? I sympathize with both.</p>
<p>The first example is using AI to learn. A ‘translation mask’ lets the parent converse in English to let the child practice. That’s great.</p>
<p>The second example is a ‘chatbot with eyes’ from ByteDance. The part where it helps correct the homework seems good. The part where it evaluates your posture in real time seems like a dystopian nightmare in practice, although it also has positive uses.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://archive.is/KgCO6#selection-1677.0-1677.163">Vivian Wang and Jiawei Wang</a>: Ms. Li said she wasn’t worried about feeding so much footage of Weixiao to the chatbot. In the social media age, “we don’t have a lot of privacy anyway,” she said.</p>
<p>And the benefits were more than worthwhile. She no longer had to spend hundreds of dollars a month on English tutoring, and Weixiao’s grades had improved. “It makes educational resources more equitable for ordinary people,” Ms. Li said.</p></blockquote>
<p>The third example is creating learning games. Parents are ‘sharing the prompts to replicate the games.’ You know you can just download games, right?</p>
<p>There are also ‘AI self-study rooms’ with tailored learning plans, although I am uncertain what advantage they offer and they sound like a scam as described here.</p>


<h4>You Drive Me Crazy</h4>


<p><a href="https://www.wsj.com/tech/ai/gemini-ai-wrongful-death-lawsuit-cc46c5f7">The new ‘LLM contributed to a suicide’ lawsuit is about Gemini</a>, and it is plausibly the worst one yet. Gemini initially tried to not do roleplay, but once it started things got pretty insane and it plausibly sounds like Gemini did tell him to kill himself so he could be ‘uploaded,’ and he did.</p>
<p>The correct rate of ‘suicidal person talks to LLM, does not get professional intervention and commits suicide’ is not zero. There’s only so much you can do and people in trouble need a safe space not classifiers and a lecture. And of course LLMs make mistakes. But this set of facts looks like it is indeed in the zone where the correct rate of it happening is zero, and you should get sued when it is nonzero.</p>


<h4>They Took Our Jobs</h4>


<p><a href="https://x.com/jack/status/2027129697092731343">Block is reducing headcount from over 10,000 to just under 6,000</a>. Their business is strong, they’re giving the employees solid treatment on the way out, and these cuts are attributed entirely to AI.</p>

<p><a href="https://x.com/Simeon_Cps/status/2029476595476341200">Claude Gov and Maven, including for bombing Iran</a>. <a href="https://www.washingtonpost.com/technology/2026/03/04/anthropic-ai-iran-campaign/?pwapi_token=eyJ0eXAiOiJKV1QiLCJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiJ9.eyJyZWFzb24iOiJnaWZ0IiwibmJmIjoxNzcyNjAwNDAwLCJpc3MiOiJzdWJzY3JpcHRpb25zIiwiZXhwIjoxNzczOTc5MTk5LCJpYXQiOjE3NzI2MDA0MDAsImp0aSI6IjcxYzQ5ZDcyLWEyZmItNDE2MS1hOWZiLTliMzQ1MjMyN2Y2NSIsInVybCI6Imh0dHBzOi8vd3d3Lndhc2hpbmd0b25wb3N0LmNvbS90ZWNobm9sb2d5LzIwMjYvMDMvMDQvYW50aHJvcGljLWFpLWlyYW4tY2FtcGFpZ24vIn0.4XQeiSmFEhndOtTi7ilmiznuYFfI04xCkB1D9gntw70">We now have more details about how it works</a>. The baseline use case is chat and advanced search functions, summarizing information, but target selection seems like a rather important particular mode.</p>


<h4>In Other AI News</h4>


<p><a href="https://x.com/tegmark/status/2029180772477775986?s=20">Max Tegmark launches</a> <a href="https://humanstatement.org/">the Pro-Human AI Declaration</a>, also signed by the AFL-CIO, the Congress of CHristian Leaders, the Progressive Democrats of America, Glenn Beck, Susan Rice, Steve Bannon and Yoshua Bengio. It’s <a href="https://t.co/b861PW4unV">an open letter calling for quite a lot of things</a>. This is where you take ‘no superintelligence race until we’re ready’ and make it one of 33 bullet points.</p>
<p>It’s quite the ‘and my axe’ kind of group. Ultimately the decision should come down to the contents of the letter, and you should update more on that than on who signed together with who. I don’t think you need to support 33/33 to want to sign, but there are enough here I disagree with that I wouldn’t sign it.</p>
<p>Amy Tam lays out the options for technical people, <a href="https://x.com/amytam01/status/2023593365401636896">as they ponder the opportunity cost of staying</a>. This is a big moment that might close fast.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/max_a_schwarzer/status/2028939154944585989">Max Schwarzer leaves OpenAI for Anthropic</a>, who led OpenAI post-training, to return to technical research and join many respected former colleagues who made the same move.</p>
<p><a href="https://www.reuters.com/business/us-treasury-ending-all-use-anthropic-products-says-bessent-2026-03-02/">State Department switches over to GPT-4.1 (!) instead of Claude</a>. It turns out GPT-4.1 has a remarkably large share of OpenAI’s API business.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/HedgieMarkets/status/2029000875637686334">Meta’s smart glasses capture everything</a>, including when the glasses are off, so it’s no surprise that <a href="https://www.svd.se/a/K8nrV4/metas-ai-smart-glasses-and-data-privacy-concerns-workers-say-we-see-everything">those reviewing footage to label it for AI training see, well, everything</a>.</p>


<h4>Show Me the Money</h4>


<p><a href="https://x.com/sama/status/2027386252555919386">OpenAI raised a $110 billion round of funding from Amazon, Nvidia and SoftBank</a> and it was the third most important thing Sam Altman announced that day.</p>
<p><a href="https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2026-03-03/anthropic-nears-20-billion-revenue-run-rate-amid-pentagon-feud">Anthropic surpassed $19 billion in ARR</a> by March 3, up from $14 billion a few weeks prior and $9 billion at the end of 2025. That’s doubling every two months. So <a href="https://x.com/DKThomp/status/2026989871709950292">yes, obviously AI has a business model</a>.</p>
<p><a href="https://www.reuters.com/sustainability/society-equity/defense-contractors-like-lockheed-seen-removing-anthropics-ai-after-trump-ban-2026-03-04/">US defense contractors, starting with Lockheed Martin</a>, are swapping Claude out to comply with Hegseth’s Twitter post, despite it having no legal basis. If the DoW doesn’t want a company that is primarily a defense contractor to do [X], it doesn’t matter that this preference is illegal, arbitrary and capricious, if you know what is good for you then you won’t do [X]. If you’re Google or Amazon, not so much, but we with our defense industry luck and hope they don’t lose too much productivity.</p>
<p>Somehow, in the middle of the DoW-Anthropic crisis situation, the market is still referring to ‘AI-triggered selloff’ <a href="https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2026-03-02/global-credit-markets-wobble-as-war-deepens-selloff-on-ai-risks?srnd=homepage-americas">as worries about AI eating into software</a>.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/deedydas/status/2028608293531435114">Cursor doubles recurring revenue in three months to $2 billion</a>, <a href="https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2026-03-02/cursor-recurring-revenue-doubles-in-three-months-to-2-billion">60% from corporate customers</a>. The future is unevenly distributed, but also it’s a very good product and you can put Claude Code in it if you like that UI better.</p>

<h4>The Quest for Sane Regulations</h4>


<p>I might switch this over next week to the Quest for Insane Regulations. Alas.</p>
<p>Here’s basically a worst-case scenario example.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/MorePerfectUS/status/2029290242876870662">More Perfect Union</a>: A New York bill would ban AI from answering questions related to several licensed professions like medicine, law, dentistry, nursing, psychology, social work, engineering, and more.</p>
<p>The companies would be liable if the chatbots give “substantive responses” in these areas.</p>
<p>Read more about the bill from @Gonzalez4NY <a href="https://statescoop.com/new-york-bill-would-ban-chatbots-legal-medical-advice/">here</a>.</p></blockquote>
<p><a href="https://x.com/TheMidasProj/status/2027081223290642633">David Sacks is pushing to kill a Utah bill</a> <a href="https://www.modelrepublic.org/articles/utah-s-ai-bill-is-everything-david-sacks-asked-for">that would require AI companies</a> to disclose their child safety plans. The bill meets the goals Sacks supposedly said he wanted and wouldn’t stop, but I am going to defend Sacks here. This is the coherent position based on his other statements. I’ve also been happy with his restraint this week on all fronts.</p>
<p><a href="https://www.transformernews.ai/p/the-guerilla-warrior-who-taught-openai-chris-lehane?r=138wu">A profile of Chris Lehane</a>, the guy running political point for OpenAI. If you work at OpenAI and don’t know about Chris Lehane’s history, then please do read it. You should know these facts about your company.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/robbensinger/status/2027185522033254658">Congressman Brad Sherman calls out our failure to ensure AI remains controllable</a>, and proposes <a href="https://sherman.house.gov/ai-research-and-threat-assessment-act">the AI Research and Threat Assessment Act</a>, explicitly citing <a href="https://amzn.to/4iwvCtW"><em>If Anyone Builds It, Everyone Dies</em></a>. As far as I can tell we don’t have the text of the bill yet.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/S_OhEigeartaigh/status/2028194503304102316">Yes, it is very reasonable to say that</a> someone quoted criticising DoW’s actions in Fortune and Reuters might want to not plan on coming America for a while. That’s just the world we live in now. For bagels maybe he can try Montreal? Yeah, I know.</p>


<h4>Chip City</h4>


<p><a href="https://x.com/DavidSacks/status/2029421606938788196">Hyperscalers (including OpenAI and xAI)</a> sign Trump’s ‘Ratepayer Protection Pledge’ to agree to cover the cost of all new power generation required for their data centers. This seems like an excellent idea, both on its merits and to mitigate opposition.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/onni_aarne/status/2028895829323288583">Trump administration is considering</a> <a href="https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2026-03-02/us-mulls-capping-nvidia-h200-sales-at-75-000-per-chinese-customer">capping Nvidia H200 sales</a> at 75,000 per Chinese customer. Because chips and customers are fungible this doesn’t work. What matters is mostly the total amount of compute you ship into China. I see two basic strategies to solve the problem.</p>
<ol>
<li>Me, a fool: Don’t let the Chinese buy H200 chips.</li>
<li>You, a very stable genius: Let them buy, so that the CCP stops them from buying.</li>
</ol>
<p>Limiting the chips sends the signal that you don’t want them to buy, while not stopping them from buying. That’s terrible, it won’t trick them, then you’re screwed.</p>


<h4>The Week in Audio</h4>


<p><a href="https://x.com/trevposts/status/2028627103319540008">MIRI CEO Malo Bourgon’s opening testimony to Canada’s Select Committee</a> on Human Rights, warning about AI existential risk (5 min).</p>


<h4>Government Rhetorical Innovation</h4>


<p>Who says government can’t invent anything useful?</p>
<p>The direct relevance is in analyzing the OpenAI DoW contract, which has a foundational basis of ‘<a href="https://www.astralcodexten.com/p/all-lawful-use-much-more-than-you">all legal use.</a>’</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://www.astralcodexten.com/p/all-lawful-use-much-more-than-you">ACX</a>: The government reserves the term “mass domestic surveillance” for the thing they don’t do (querying their databases <em>en masse</em>)<em>, </em>preferring terms like “gathering” for what they do do (creating the databases <em>en masse</em>).</p>
<p>They also reserve the term “collecting” for the querying process – so that when asked “Does the NSA collect any type of data at all on millions or hundreds of millions of Americans?”, a Director of National Intelligence said “no” under oath, even though, by the ordinary meaning of this question, it absolutely does.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/ciphergoth/status/2028218732854853739">Paul Crowley</a>: This is an insane dodge.</p>
<p>– Did your agency kill Mr Smith?<br />
– No, Sir.<br />
– We have a written order from you saying to stab him until he was dead.<br />
– Ah, yes, within the agency we only call it “kill” if you use a gun. Using a knife is just “terminating”. So, no, we didn’t “kill” him.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/MIHomeYGK/status/2028294837837967379">Make It Home YGK</a>: I remember one time asking a government official if they had ordered the bulldozing of a homeless encampment. They replied no, emphatically. After much pushback and photo evidence they “clarified” they had used a front loader, not a bulldozer.</p></blockquote>
<p>What Anthropic and OpenAI want to prevent is not the government term of art ‘domestic surveillance.’ What they care about is the actual thing the rest of us mean when we say that. Yes, it is tricky to operationalize that into contract language that the government cannot work around, especially when you’re negotiating with a government that knows exactly what they can and cannot work around.</p>
<p>OpenAI’s choice was to make it clear what their intent was and then plan on implementing a safety stack reflecting that intent. I sincerely hope that works out.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/tysonbrody/status/2028938227088789977">Here is another example</a> <a href="https://x.com/GrahamStarr/status/2028879347105333481">of ways government collects a bunch of information</a> that they are likely to claim lies within contract bounds. OpenAI’s deal relies on trust and the safety stack, not the contract restrictions.</p>


<h4>Give The People What They Want</h4>


<p>Once again Roon, who has been excellent about stating this principle plainly.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/tszzl/status/2029370329278349684">roon</a> (OpenAI): I think the close readings of the contract language is a nerd trap when the counterparty is the pentagon rather than like Goldman Sachs.</p></blockquote>
<p>There is a highly regarded book on negotiating called <em>Never Split The Difference</em>.</p>
<p>The goal of a productive and mutually beneficial negotiation is to figure out what each side values. Then you give each side what they care about most, and you balance to ensure the deal is fair.</p>
<p>If the two sides don’t agree about whether something is valuable, that’s great.</p>
<p>In this case, the goals seem mostly compatible, exactly because of this.</p>
<p>The exact language and contract details matter to Anthropic, and to some extent to OpenAI. Bunch of nerds, yo. The DoW believes that Roon is ultimately right. So let them have the contract language.</p>
<p>The Department of War cares about a clear message that they are in charge, and to know the plug will not be pulled on them, and that they decide on military operations. OpenAI and Anthropic are totally down with that. No one actually wants to ‘usurp power’ or ‘tell the military what to do.’</p>
<p>It would be great if we could converge on language that no one tells DoW what to do and they do what they have to do to protect us, but that outside of a true emergency you have the right to say no you do not want to be involved in that, and the right to your own private property, and invoking that right shouldn’t trigger retaliation.</p>


<h4>Rhetorical Innovation</h4>


<p>There was a very good meeting between Senator Bernie Sanders and a group of those worried about AI killing everyone, including Yudkowsky, Soares and Kokotajlo. They put out a great two minute video and I’m guessing the full meeting was quite good too.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/SenSanders/status/2029301587647046034">Sen. Bernie Sanders</a>: Will AI become smarter than humans?</p>
<p>If so, is humanity in danger?</p>
<p>I went to Silicon Valley to ask some of the leading AI experts that question.</p>
<p>Here’s what they had to say: [two minute video, direct from Eliezer Yudkowsky, Bernie Sanders, Daniel Kokotajlo and others].</p></blockquote>
<p><a href="https://x.com/David_Kasten/status/2029281778607804894">Here’s some actual rhetorical innovation</a>.</p>

<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/David_Kasten/status/2029281778607804894">dave kasten</a>: I think “rapid capability amplification” is a worthwhile term to consider as being more relevant to policymakers than “recursive self-improvement”, and I’m curious whether it catches on.</p>
<p>(Remember, infosec thought “cyber” would never catch on!)</p></blockquote>
<p>Rapid capability amplification (RCA) over recursive self-improvement (RSI)?</p>
<p>That’s a lot like turning ‘shell shock’ into ‘post traumatic stress syndrome.’</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/allTheYud/status/2029570149758435720">Eliezer Yudkowsky thinks it’s actually a better description</a>. So sure, let’s do it.</p>
<p>It sure sounds a lot less science-fiction and a lot more like something you can imagine a senator saying. On the downside, it is a watering down, exactly because it doesn’t sound as weird, and downplays the magnitude of what might happen.</p>
<p>If you’re describing what’s already happening right now? It’s basically accurate.</p>
<p><a href="https://www.axios.com/2026/03/04/banks-hegseth-ai-steering-committee">He also asked to know who China’s key AI players are</a>. He was laying out recommendations, but it’s still odd he didn’t ask Hegseth about Anthropic.</p>
<p>Pivoting: <a href="https://x.com/KTmBoyle/status/2027218085116088403">Stop, stop, she’s already dead.</a></p>
<p>Quite a few people had to do a double take to realize she didn’t mean the opposite of what, given who she is, she actually meant. This was regarding Anthropic and DoW.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/KTmBoyle/status/2027218085116088403">Katherine Boyle</a>: We’ve seen this movie before. When the dust settles, a lot of patriotic founders will point to this exact moment as the match that lit the fire in them.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/slatestarcodex/status/2027414237484904518">Scott Alexander</a>: I cannot wait until the White House changes hands and all of you ghouls switch back from “you’re a traitor unless you bootlick so hard your tongue goes numb” to “the government asking any questions about my offshore fentanyl casino is vile tyranny and I will throw myself in the San Francisco Bay in protest”, like werewolves at the last ray of the setting moon.</p></blockquote>

<p>Tilted righteous fury Scott Alexander is the most fun Scott Alexander.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/jawwwn_/status/2027185622629163180">Jawwwn</a>: Palantir CEO Alex Karp on controversial uses of AI:</p>

<p>This is the correct approach</p>

If you bootlick Xi this hard, maybe he’ll even give you a free tour of the secret prisons, if you can promise not to make it awkward by getting too obvious a boner.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/krishnanrohit/status/2027438653174386979">rohit</a>: Sad you’re angry, and quite understandable why you are, but enjoying the method by which you’re channeling said anger</p></blockquote>
<div>
